\int_{0}^{y}G_{\varepsilon}(d,s)\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}s\leq c_{1}-c_{2}y,

for all
y\in[0,\varepsilon^{-1}\pi]
. We finally come to estimate the absolute value of the
q
-Gamma function as

(B.22)

\begin{split}\left|\Gamma_{q}(d+\mathrm{i}y)\right|&=\Gamma_{q}(d)e^{\int_{0}^{y}\frac{\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}}{\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}s}\log\left|\Gamma_{q}(d+\mathrm{i}s)\right|\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}s}\\
&\leq\Gamma_{q}(d)e^{\int_{0}^{y}G_{\varepsilon}(d,s)\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}s}\\
&\leq\Gamma_{q}(d)e^{c_{1}-c_{2}y}\end{split}

and this completes the proof.
∎

Lemma B.7
.

Let
\varepsilon>0
,
q=e^{-\varepsilon}
and fix
a,b\in\mathbb{R}
, with
a\notin\mathbb{Z}_{\geq 0}
. Then, there exists a constant
C
depending only on
a,b
, such that

(B.23)

\left|\frac{\Gamma_{q}(a+\mathrm{i}y)}{\Gamma_{q}(b+\mathrm{i}y)}\right|\leq C(1+|y|)^{a-b},

for all
\varepsilon>0
and
y\in[-\varepsilon^{-1}\pi,\varepsilon^{-1}\pi]
.

Proof.

It suffices to prove the inequality (
B.23
) for
1<a<b
, as the general case can be easily recovered using the functional equation (
B.13
).

Assume
1<a<b
. By Lagrange’s mean value theorem we have

(B.24)

\begin{split}\log\left|\Gamma_{q}(a+\mathrm{i}y)\right|-\log\left|\Gamma_{q}(b+\mathrm{i}y)\right|&=(a-b)\frac{\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}}{\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}a}\log\left|\Gamma_{q}(a+\mathrm{i}y)\right|\bigg|_{a=a_{*}}\\
&=(a-b)\left(-\log\left(1-e^{-\varepsilon}\right)+\sum_{k\geq 0}\varphi_{\varepsilon}(a_{*}+k,y)\right),\end{split}

for some
a_{*}\in[a,b]
, where we have defined the functions

(B.25)

\varphi_{\varepsilon}(x,y)\coloneqq-\frac{\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}}{\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}x}\log\left|1-e^{-\varepsilon(x+\mathrm{i}y)}\right|=\frac{\varepsilon(1-e^{\varepsilon x}\cos(\varepsilon y))}{1+e^{2\varepsilon x}-2e^{\varepsilon x}\cos(\varepsilon y)}.

Computing the derivative

(B.26)

\frac{\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}}{\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}x}\varphi_{\varepsilon}(x,y)=\frac{\varepsilon^{2}e^{2\varepsilon x}((e^{\varepsilon x}+e^{-\varepsilon x})\cos(\varepsilon y)-2)}{(1+e^{2\varepsilon x}-2e^{\varepsilon x}\cos(\varepsilon y))^{2}},

we find that the function
k\mapsto\varphi_{\varepsilon}(x+k,y)
has critical point at

(B.27)

k_{*}=-x+\varepsilon^{-1}\mathrm{arccosh}\left(\frac{1}{\cos(\varepsilon y)}\right),

where by convention we fix
k_{*}=-\infty
if
\cos(\varepsilon y)<0
. Restricting to values
k>0
and
x>1
, we see that
\varphi_{\varepsilon}(x+k,y)
is increasing in the interval
k\in[0,k_{*}\vee 0)
and decreasing for
k\geq k_{*}\vee 0
, so that we can estimate the summation in the second line of (
B.24
), using
lemma

B.5
as

(B.28)

\begin{split}\sum_{k}\varphi_{\varepsilon}(x+k,y)&\geq\left(\int_{-1}^{[k_{*}]}+\int_{[k_{*}]+1}^{\infty}\right)\varphi_{\varepsilon}(x+k,y)\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}k+\varphi_{\varepsilon}(x+[k_{*}],y)\\
&=\left(\int_{-1}^{\infty}-\int_{[k_{*}]}^{[k_{*}]+1}\right)\varphi_{\varepsilon}(x+k,y)\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}k+\varphi_{\varepsilon}(x+[k_{*}],y)\\
&=\log\left|1-e^{-\varepsilon(x-1+\mathrm{i}y)}\right|-\int_{k_{*}}^{k_{*}+1}\varphi_{\varepsilon}(x+k,y)\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}k+\varphi_{\varepsilon}(x+[k_{*}],y),\end{split}

if
k_{*}>0
and

(B.29)

\sum_{k=0}^{\infty}\varphi_{\varepsilon}(x+k,y)\geq\int_{0}^{\infty}\varphi_{\varepsilon}(x+k,y)\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}k=\log\left|1-e^{-\varepsilon(x+\mathrm{i}y)}\right|,

if
k_{*}\leq 0
. Since the function
\varphi_{\varepsilon}(x,y)
is bounded in absolute value for
x>1
and any
y
, as it can be seen from the explicit expression (
B.25
), we can conclude that

(B.30)

\sum_{k=0}^{\infty}\varphi_{\varepsilon}(x+k,y)\geq\log\left|1-e^{-\varepsilon(x-1+\mathrm{i}y)}\right|+C,

for some constant
C
independent of
\varepsilon,y
. Substituting this bound in (
B.24
) we obtain

(B.31)

\begin{split}\log\left|\frac{\Gamma_{q}(a+\mathrm{i}y)}{\Gamma_{q}(b+\mathrm{i}y)}\right|&\leq(a-b)\left(\log\left|\frac{1-e^{-\varepsilon(a_{*}-1+\mathrm{i}y)}}{1-e^{-\varepsilon}}\right|+C\right)\leq C^{\prime}+(a-b)\log(1+|y|),\end{split}

for some other constant
C^{\prime}
independent of
\varepsilon,y
, which proves (
B.23
).
∎

Making use of the estimates developed throughout this subsection we are finally able to prove
theorem

5.4
.

B.2.
Useful bounds for the proof of
theorem

5.10

In order to prove
theorem

5.10
we need a number of bounds on ratio of
q
-Gamma function that add to results already established in the previous subsection. The main technical result is given by the following proposition.

Proposition B.8
.

Let
q=e^{-\varepsilon}
, for
\varepsilon>0
and consider complex numbers
Z=-d+\mathrm{i}u,W=-d+\mathrm{i}v
with
d\in(0,1/2)
. Then, there exists a
C
independent of
u,v,\varepsilon
, such that we have

(B.32)

\left|\frac{\Gamma_{q}(-2Z)\Gamma_{q}(-2W)\Gamma_{q}(1+Z+W)\Gamma_{q}(-W-Z)}{\Gamma_{q}(W-Z)\Gamma_{q}(1+Z-W)}\right|\\
\leq C(1+2|u|)^{-2d-1/2}(1+2|v|)^{-2d-1/2},

for all
u,v\in[-\varepsilon^{-1}\pi,\varepsilon^{-1}\pi]
.

We prove (
B.32
) by combining a number of bounds on ratio of
q
-Gamma functions, that we show separately in the following lemmas.

Lemma B.9
.

Let
\varepsilon>0
,
q=e^{-\varepsilon}
. Then, we have

(B.33)

\left|\frac{\Gamma_{q}(\frac{1}{2}+\mathrm{i}(a+b))\Gamma_{q}(\frac{1}{2}+\mathrm{i}a)}{\Gamma_{q}(\frac{1}{2}+\mathrm{i}b)}\right|\leq C

for all
a,b\in[0,\varepsilon^{-1}\pi]
, where
C
is a constant independent of
a,b,\varepsilon
.

Proof.

Based on the value of
a,b
we distinguish five regions, visualized in
fig.

6
(a) and we prove the desired inequality in each of these regions.

(
\cdot
) When
0\leq a\leq b\leq\frac{\pi}{\varepsilon}
, we have
|\Gamma_{q}(\frac{1}{2}+\mathrm{i}a)|\leq|\Gamma_{q}(\frac{1}{2}+\mathrm{i}b)|
and (
B.33
) holds since is
\Gamma_{q}(\frac{1}{2}+\mathrm{i}(a+b))
bounded; this case correspond to the gray area in
fig.

6
(a).

(
\cdot
) When
a<b
and
a+b\leq\frac{\pi}{\varepsilon}
, corresponding to the yellow region, we have
|\Gamma_{q}(\frac{1}{2}+\mathrm{i}(a+b))|\leq|\Gamma_{q}(\frac{1}{2}+\mathrm{i}b)|
and again (
B.33
) holds.

(
\cdot
) The case
a<b,a+b>\frac{\pi}{\varepsilon}
and
b\leq\frac{2\pi}{\varepsilon}-a-b
, corresponds to the violet region. Since

(B.34)

\Gamma_{q}(\frac{1}{2}+\mathrm{i}(a+b))=\Gamma_{q}(\frac{1}{2}+\mathrm{i}(\frac{2\pi}{\varepsilon}-a-b))

as in the previous case we have
|\Gamma_{q}(\frac{1}{2}+\mathrm{i}(a+b))|\leq|\Gamma_{q}(\frac{1}{2}+\mathrm{i}b)|
and (
B.33
) holds.

(
\cdot
) The red region corresponds to choice
a+b>\frac{\pi}{\varepsilon}
and
a<\frac{2\pi}{\varepsilon}-a-b<b
. Here we find that the denominator in (
B.33
) is smaller than either factors in the numerator and in this case the desired inequality will be proven by carefully taking into account each term in the expression. Using again (
B.34
), we write

(B.35)

\log\left|\frac{\Gamma_{q}(\frac{1}{2}+\mathrm{i}(a+b))\Gamma_{q}(\frac{1}{2}+\mathrm{i}a)}{\Gamma_{q}(\frac{1}{2}+\mathrm{i}b)}\right|=\log|\Gamma_{q}(\frac{1}{2})|+\left(\int_{0}^{a}-\int_{\frac{2\pi}{\varepsilon}-a-b}^{b}\right)\Phi_{\varepsilon}(y)\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}y,

where

(B.36)

\Phi_{\varepsilon}(y)=\frac{\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}}{\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}y}\log|\Gamma_{q}(\frac{1}{2}+\mathrm{i}y)|.

Since
\Phi_{\varepsilon}(y)
is negative for
y\in(0,\frac{\pi}{\varepsilon})
we can estimate the second integral in the right hand side of (
B.35
) by enlarging the integration domain as

(B.37)

\int_{\frac{2\pi}{\varepsilon}-a-b}^{b}\Phi_{\varepsilon}(y)\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}y\geq\int_{\frac{\pi}{\varepsilon}-a}^{\frac{\pi}{\varepsilon}}\Phi_{\varepsilon}(y)\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}y.

Plugging the previous inequality in (
B.35
), we find that (
B.33
) holds, for instance, if

(B.38)

\int_{0}^{a}\Phi_{\varepsilon}(y)\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}y\leq\int_{\frac{\pi}{\varepsilon}-a}^{\frac{\pi}{\varepsilon}}\Phi_{\varepsilon}(y)\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}y,\qquad\text{for all }a\in[0,\frac{2\pi}{3\varepsilon}]

and this is what we aim to show. We make use of the estimate (
B.19
) to bound integrands in both sides of (
B.38
). In particular, as shown in
fig.

6
(b), we see that

(B.39)

\Phi_{\varepsilon}(y)\leq G_{\varepsilon}(1/2,y_{*})\left[\mathbf{1}_{[0,y_{*}]}(y)\frac{y}{y_{*}}+\mathbf{1}_{(y_{*},\frac{\pi}{\varepsilon}]}(y)\frac{\frac{\pi}{\varepsilon}-y}{\frac{\pi}{\varepsilon}-y_{*}}\right],

where
y_{*}=\varepsilon^{-1}\arccos(e^{-\varepsilon/2})
is the critical point of
G_{\varepsilon}(1/2,y)
and

(B.40)

\Phi_{\varepsilon}(y)\geq G_{\varepsilon}(-1/2,y)\geq-\varepsilon\frac{\frac{\pi}{\varepsilon}-y}{2e^{-\varepsilon/2}},\qquad\text{for }y\in[\frac{\pi}{3\varepsilon},\frac{\pi}{\varepsilon}].

The first inequality follows from convexity of
G_{\varepsilon}(1/2,y)
, while the second can be derived bounding the derivative
\frac{\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}}{\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}y}G_{\varepsilon}(1/2,y)
in the indicated region of
y
.
In order to prove (
B.38
), we can improve such inequality by replacing the integrands with the right hand sides of (
B.39
) and (
B.40
). Computing the integrals we find that (
B.38
) holds, if

(B.41)

G_{\varepsilon}(1/2,y_{*})\left[\mathbf{1}_{[0,y_{*}]}(a)\frac{a^{2}}{2y_{*}}+\mathbf{1}_{(y_{*},\frac{\pi}{\varepsilon}]}(a)\frac{\pi(2a-y_{*})-\varepsilon a^{2}}{2(\pi-\varepsilon y_{*})}\right]\leq-\frac{a^{2}\varepsilon e^{-\varepsilon/2}}{2},

for all
a\in[0,\frac{2\pi}{3\varepsilon}]
. The previous polynomial inequality can at this point easily verified for
\varepsilon
small enough, noticing that
y_{*}=\varepsilon^{-1/2}+O(\varepsilon^{1/2})
and
G_{\varepsilon}(1/2,y_{*})=-\frac{\pi}{2}+O(\varepsilon^{1/2})
. This proves (
B.38
) and hence (
B.33
) for the region
a+b>\frac{\pi}{\varepsilon}
and
a<\frac{2\pi}{\varepsilon}-a-b<b
.

(
\cdot
) Lastly we consider the blue region in
fig.

6
(a), corresponding to choices
a+b>\frac{\pi}{\varepsilon}
and
\frac{2\pi}{\varepsilon}-a-b<a<b
. We use once again the integral expression (
B.35
) and given our conditions on
a,b
we need to estimate the difference

(B.42)

\left(\int_{0}^{\frac{2\pi}{\varepsilon}-a-b}-\int_{a}^{b}\right)\Phi_{\varepsilon}(y)\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}y.

As a function of
b
, (
B.42
) is an increasing function in the region
b\in[\frac{2\pi}{3\varepsilon},\frac{\pi}{\varepsilon}]
and therefore we have

(B.43)

\left(\int_{0}^{\frac{2\pi}{\varepsilon}-a-b}-\int_{a}^{b}\right)\Phi_{\varepsilon}(y)\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}y\leq\left(\int_{0}^{\frac{\pi}{\varepsilon}-a}-\int_{a}^{\frac{\pi}{\varepsilon}}\right)\Phi_{\varepsilon}(y)\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}y.

By a change of variable
a^{\prime}=\frac{\pi}{\varepsilon}-a\in[0,\frac{\pi}{2\varepsilon}]
, we recognize that the term in the right hand side is negative as a result of (
B.38
) and this shows (
B.33
) for the region of parameters
a,b
under consideration, concluding the proof.
∎

(a)

(b)

Figure 6.
On the left panel we see a breakdown of regions for parameters
a,b
considered in the proof of
lemma

B.9
. On the right panel we see a plot of the function
G_{\varepsilon}(d,y)
defined in (
B.20
) for
d=\pm 1/2
and a display of inequalities (
B.19
).

Lemma B.10
.

Let
\varepsilon>0,q=e^{-\varepsilon}
. Then, we have

(B.44)

\left|\frac{\Gamma_{q}(1+\mathrm{i}a)}{\Gamma_{q^{2}}(1+\mathrm{i}a)}\right|\leq 1,

for all
a\in[0,\varepsilon^{-1}\pi]
.

Proof.

We write

(B.45)

\begin{split}\log\left|\frac{\Gamma_{q}(1+\mathrm{i}a)}{\Gamma_{q^{2}}(1+\mathrm{i}a)}\right|&=\int_{0}^{a}\frac{\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}}{\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}y}\log\left|\frac{\Gamma_{q}(1+\mathrm{i}y)}{\Gamma_{q^{2}}(1+\mathrm{i}y)}\right|\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}y\\
&=\int_{0}^{a}\sum_{k\geq 1}\left(\phi_{\varepsilon}(k,y)-\phi_{2\varepsilon}(k,y)\right)\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}y,\end{split}

where the function
\phi_{\varepsilon}
was introduced in (
B.17
). Evaluating explicitly the difference of the two
\phi_{\varepsilon}
functions in the integrand we find

(B.46)

\phi_{\varepsilon}(k,y)-\phi_{2\varepsilon}(k,y)=-\frac{\varepsilon e^{-\varepsilon k}\sin(\varepsilon y)}{1+e^{-2\varepsilon k}+2e^{-\varepsilon k}\cos(\varepsilon y)},

which is negative for
0<y<\varepsilon^{-1}\pi
. This proves the desired inequality.
∎

Lemma B.11
.

Let
\varepsilon>0,q=e^{-\varepsilon}
. Then, we have

(B.47)

\left|\frac{\Gamma_{q}(\frac{1}{2}+\mathrm{i}a)^{2}}{\Gamma_{q}(\frac{1}{2}+2\mathrm{i}a)}\right|\leq C,

for all
a\in[0,\varepsilon^{-1}\pi/2]
, where
C
is a constant independent of
a,\varepsilon
.

Proof.

We use the duplication formula for the
q
-Gamma function

(B.48)

\Gamma_{q}(2z)=\mathsf{c}_{q,z}\Gamma_{q^{2}}(z)\Gamma_{q^{2}}(\frac{1}{2}+z),

where
\mathsf{c}_{q,z}=\frac{(1+q)^{2z-1}}{(1-q^{2})^{1/2}(q^{2},-q;q)_{\infty}}
and we have
\mathsf{c}_{q,z}\xrightarrow[q\to 1]{}\frac{2^{2z-1}}{\sqrt{\pi}}
.
Through simple algebraic manipulations we rewrite the ratio in the left hand side of (
B.47
) as

(B.49)

\begin{split}\frac{\Gamma_{q}(\frac{1}{2}+\mathrm{i}a)^{2}}{\Gamma_{q}(\frac{1}{2}+2\mathrm{i}a)}&=C_{q,a}\frac{\Gamma_{q}(\frac{1}{2}+\mathrm{i}a)^{2}}{\Gamma_{q^{2}}(\frac{1}{4}+\mathrm{i}a)\Gamma_{q^{2}}(\frac{3}{4}+\mathrm{i}a)}\\
&=C_{q,a}\left(\frac{\Gamma_{q}(\frac{1}{2}+\mathrm{i}a)}{\Gamma_{q}(1+\mathrm{i}a)}\right)^{2}\left(\frac{\Gamma_{q}(1+\mathrm{i}a)}{\Gamma_{q^{2}}(1+\mathrm{i}a)}\right)^{2}\frac{\Gamma_{q^{2}}(1+\mathrm{i}a)}{\Gamma_{q^{2}}(\frac{1}{4}+\mathrm{i}a)}\frac{\Gamma_{q^{2}}(1+\mathrm{i}a)}{\Gamma_{q^{2}}(\frac{3}{4}+\mathrm{i}a)},\end{split}

for some constant
C_{q,a}
, which is absolutely bounded in
q
. Each term in the right hand side can be bounded in absolute value through
lemma

B.7
and
lemma

B.10
and the resulting inequality yields (
B.47
).
∎

Lemma B.12
.

Let
\varepsilon>0
,
q=e^{-\varepsilon}
. Then, we have

(B.50)

\left|\frac{\Gamma_{q}(\frac{1}{2}+\mathrm{i}(b-a))\Gamma_{q}(\frac{1}{2}+\mathrm{i}a)}{\Gamma_{q}(\frac{1}{2}+\mathrm{i}b)}\right|\leq C

for all
a,b\in[0,\varepsilon^{-1}\pi]
, where
C
is a constant independent of
a,b,\varepsilon
.

Proof.

When
b<a
(
B.50
) is straightforward, as we have
|\Gamma_{q}(\frac{1}{2}+\mathrm{i}a)|<|\Gamma_{q}(\frac{1}{2}+\mathrm{i}b)|
and
\Gamma_{q}(\frac{1}{2}+\mathrm{i}(b-a))
is bounded. Therefore, from now on, we assume
b>a
. Using the same notation introduced in the proof of
lemma

B.9
, we write

(B.51)

\log\left|\frac{\Gamma_{q}(\frac{1}{2}+\mathrm{i}(b-a))\Gamma_{q}(\frac{1}{2}+\mathrm{i}a)}{\Gamma_{q}(\frac{1}{2}+\mathrm{i}b)}\right|=\log|\Gamma_{q}(\frac{1}{2})|+\left(\int_{0}^{a}-\int_{b-a}^{b}\right)\Phi_{\varepsilon}(y)\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}y.

A study of the derivatives of the right hand side shows that that (
B.51
) has a global maximum along the line
b=2a
. This shows that

(B.52)

\left|\frac{\Gamma_{q}(\frac{1}{2}+\mathrm{i}(b-a))\Gamma_{q}(\frac{1}{2}+\mathrm{i}a)}{\Gamma_{q}(\frac{1}{2}+\mathrm{i}b)}\right|\leq\left|\frac{\Gamma_{q}(\frac{1}{2}+\mathrm{i}a)^{2}}{\Gamma_{q}(\frac{1}{2}+\mathrm{i}2a)}\right|,

for all
0\leq a<b<\frac{\pi}{\varepsilon}
and invoking
lemma

B.11
we conclude the proof.
∎

We are finally ready to prove the main technical bound of the subsection.

Proof of
proposition

B.8
.

We rewrite the left hand side of (
B.32
) as

(B.53)

\left|\frac{\Gamma_{q}(-2Z)}{\Gamma_{q}(\frac{1}{2}-2\mathrm{i}u)}\frac{\Gamma_{q}(-2W)}{\Gamma_{q}(\frac{1}{2}-2\mathrm{i}v)}\frac{\Gamma_{q}(1+Z+W)}{\Gamma_{q}(\frac{1}{2}+\mathrm{i}(u+v))}\frac{\Gamma_{q}(-Z-W)}{\Gamma_{q}(\frac{1}{2}-\mathrm{i}(u+v))}\frac{\Gamma_{q}(\frac{1}{2}-\mathrm{i}(u-v))}{\Gamma_{q}(W-Z)}\frac{\Gamma_{q}(\frac{1}{2}+\mathrm{i}(u-v))}{\Gamma_{q}(1+Z-W)}\right|\\
\times\left|\frac{\Gamma_{q}(\frac{1}{2}-2\mathrm{i}u)\Gamma_{q}(\frac{1}{2}-2\mathrm{i}v)\Gamma_{q}(\frac{1}{2}+\mathrm{i}(u+v))\Gamma_{q}(\frac{1}{2}-\mathrm{i}(u+v))}{\Gamma_{q}(\frac{1}{2}-\mathrm{i}(u-v))\Gamma_{q}(\frac{1}{2}+\mathrm{i}(u-v))}\right|.

This manipulation is convenient, since, through
lemma

B.7
we can estimate the ratio of
q
-Gamma functions with arguments having equal imaginary parts. Using (
B.23
) repeatedly, we obtain

\left|\frac{\Gamma_{q}(-2Z)}{\Gamma_{q}(\frac{1}{2}-2\mathrm{i}u)}\frac{\Gamma_{q}(-2W)}{\Gamma_{q}(\frac{1}{2}-2\mathrm{i}v)}\frac{\Gamma_{q}(1+Z+W)}{\Gamma_{q}(\frac{1}{2}+\mathrm{i}(u+v))}\frac{\Gamma_{q}(-Z-W)}{\Gamma_{q}(\frac{1}{2}-\mathrm{i}(u+v))}\frac{\Gamma_{q}(\frac{1}{2}-\mathrm{i}(u-v))}{\Gamma_{q}(W-Z)}\frac{\Gamma_{q}(\frac{1}{2}+\mathrm{i}(u-v))}{\Gamma_{q}(1+Z-W)}\right|\\
\leq C(1+2|u|)^{-2d-1/2}(1+2|v|)^{-2d-1/2}.

In order to estimate the factor in the second line of (
B.53
), we utilize the change of variables
a=u+v,b=u-v
and write it in the slighly simpler expression

(B.54)

\left|\frac{\Gamma_{q}\left(\frac{1}{2}+\mathrm{i}(a+b)\right)\Gamma_{q}\left(\frac{1}{2}+\mathrm{i}a\right)^{2}\Gamma_{q}\left(\frac{1}{2}+\mathrm{i}(b-a)\right)}{\Gamma_{q}\left(\frac{1}{2}+\mathrm{i}b\right)^{2}}\right|.

Such function is symmetric with respect to the transformations
(a,b)\mapsto(\pm a,\pm b)
, so that we only need to show that it is bounded for
a,b\in[0,\varepsilon^{-1}\pi]
, in which case such property follows from
lemmas

B.9
and
B.12
. This completes the proof.
∎
